# DDN conformance audit — code against `docs/vrp-problem-definition.md` v0.12

**Date:** 19 September 2026  
**Commit audited:** `96b5f2f` (working tree clean before and after)  
**Spec:** `docs/vrp-problem-definition.md` Draft v0.12, 16 September 2026  
**Rules:** `CLAUDE.md`  
**Method:** 28 independent agents traced requirements to code and tests; every IMPLEMENTED/PARTIAL claim was then given to a separate adversarial agent instructed to refute it. Refuted rows are downgraded below and marked ⚠. Nine one-line mutations were applied to production code and the suite re-run (§4).

> **Auditor's note on scope.** This audit reads and reports; it changes nothing. The only
> writes were the nine temporary mutations of §4, each reverted immediately and each followed
> by a clean-tree assertion. `git status` is clean and `HEAD` is unchanged at `96b5f2f`.

> **A caveat on one input.** The brief names `docs/claude-code-task-prompts.md` as the prompts
> used to build the repository. **That file does not exist** — there is no file matching
> `*task-prompt*` anywhere in the tree. Nothing in this audit is traced to it. The brief also
> names `solver_adapter.check_hard_constraints`; there is no such symbol. The real entry points
> are `check_route_constraints` and `check_day_constraints`, and §3 reports on both.

> **Concurrency disclosure.** The mutation runs of §4 overlapped in time with the tracing agents.
> One agent observed a transient mutation in the working tree and reported it. Its conclusion
> (that `postcheck.py:246` is untested) independently matches mutation **M6** below, but readers
> should know the two observations are not fully independent.

> **Addendum — the spec moved to v0.13 while this audit was being written.** The working tree
> carries an uncommitted `docs/vrp-problem-definition.md` at **Draft v0.13**, whose revision entry
> reads *"Corrections from conformance audit"*. What follows is a snapshot of commit `96b5f2f`
> against **v0.12** and is deliberately not restated against v0.13. For a reader reconciling them:
>
> - **Resolved — §5.2.6's three extra edges.** v0.13 draws `Transfer requested → Ready`,
>   `Transfer requested → Return run` and `Ready → Return run`. The three `CONTRADICTS` rows in the
>   lifecycle group, and the matching drift row, are answered: the code was ahead of the document.
> - **Resolved — §10's arithmetic.** §10 now states 10 discrepancies and shows
>   `4,800 − 200 − 40 − 10 = 4,550`. **§5's headline inverts:** `peak_day.py`'s `DISPUTED = 10` was
>   right and the document has been corrected to it.
> - **Resolved — §10's second self-contradiction.** D2–D6 now carry 130 unassigned; the totals move
>   to 150 unassigned, 2,950 dispatched, 2,750 delivered, tomorrow's pool 4,820, ~1,800 unassigned.
> - **Partly answered** — §7.1's heading now reads "Hard constraints (thirteen)", and §5.1.6 states
>   a late-request rule. **Gap 6 stands**: it is about the *code's* prose, which still says eleven.
> - **Sharpened — gap 7.** §5.5 now says explicitly that depot rejections join the *following*
>   evening's run, which is what `ddn/simulation/day.py:225,367` does not do.
> - **New.** `ddn/assumptions.py`'s outcome rates were read off §10's old 2,880/3,080 split; v0.13's
>   2,750/2,950 supersedes them. The caveat above about `docs/claude-code-task-prompts.md` is also
>   out of date — that file now exists in the working tree.
>
> Against the v0.13 working tree the suite is red (5 failures): §13's subsections were renumbered
> to 14.x, `peak_day.py` still quotes §10's old "12 discrepancies", and `CLAUDE.md` no longer
> states the test count. None of those are findings of this audit; all three are in uncommitted
> edits that postdate it.

---

## 1. Traceability matrix

One row per requirement. **Status** is post-refutation; ⚠ marks a row an adversarial reviewer
downgraded, with its reason in the evidence column. `IMPLEMENTED` requires code *and* a test
that fails if the code is removed; `PARTIAL` is code without a discriminating test, a test
without code, or a fallback where the spec asked for the feature.

**Notes — §7.1 Hard constraints.** DOC DEFECTS FOUND (prose disagreeing with the code it describes — all currently green): 1. ddn/solver_adapter/postcheck.py:43 says "§7.1's eleven bullets, transcribed" immediately above thirteen constants, and cites "tests/test_postcheck.py" as the test that reads the section. That file DOES NOT EXIST — `ls tests/` has no test_postcheck.py; the tests that do this live in tests/test_solver_adapter.py:118 and :139. The module docstring at :7 likewise says "Seven bullets are answerable from one solve. Four are not" (= eleven) and at :16 "vrp.verify checks seventeen invariants". ddn/solver_adapter/__init__.py:4 repeats "§7.1's eleven bullets", and check_day_constraints' docstring at :402 says "The four §7.1 bullets" while the function now checks five (the two transfer bullets were added). v0.12 took §7.1 from eleven to thirteen and the prose was not updated with it. 2. CLAUDE.md:24-30 describes postcheck as "in two halves because §7.1 is: check_route_constraints answers the seven bullets one solve can see, check_day_constraints the four that span stages" — seven + four = eleven, against thirteen bullets. CLAUDE.md also still calls the spec "Draft v0.10"; docs/vrp-problem-definition.md:3 says Draft v0.12. TRANSIENT WORKING-TREE MUTATION OBSERVED: partway through this audit `git diff` showed ddn/solver_adapter/postcheck.py:246 carrying `if False and start is not None and first != start`, disabling the start-at-home-facility check. With that mutation in place `uv run pytest tests/test_solver_adapter.py -q` still reported **25 passed**. The tree reverted to clean shortly after (git status is clean now, and I made no edits — this audit was read-only). I am reporting it because it is direct empirical evidence for the 7.1-route-home-and-shift row: that check is not covered by a test that fails when it is inverted. Confusingly, one `git status --porcelain` printed "M ddn/linehaul/circuit.py" while the accompanying `git diff` showed only the postcheck.py hunk; I could not reconcile that and did not investigate further, since investigating would have meant writing. **Notes — §7.2 Soft constraints.** Scope note: the task said to read §7.2 at lines 331-341; in this working tree (Draft v0.12, docs/vrp-problem-definition.md) the heading is at line 330 and the seven bullets are lines 333-339. All seven are enumerated above, with five split into a covered half and an uncovered half rather than collapsed. Shape of the answer: §7.2 is the weakest-covered section I looked at. Only bullet 4 (fleet reallocation) is implemented as a soft constraint in the sense §7.2 means — something is minimised and the residual cost is counted and surfaced. Bullets 2, 3, 5 and 6 are covered not by penalties but by policies that make the bad case structurally impossible or by exception reporting: the van departs at its deadline so it cannot leave early; postponed envelopes are put back in tomorrow's pool; SLA-today envelopes are forced in as a §6.1 hard constraint; transfers are attempted on tonight's circuit and otherwise declined with a reason. That is defensible engineering and the module docstrings argue it explicitly, but it means there is no §7.2 penalty machinery anywhere: grep for `penalt\|penalis\|penaliz\|soft_max` across ddn/ returns four lines, all comments or the unused constant. Drift worth flagging: ddn/assumptions.py:46 STOPS_PER_ROUTE_SOFT_MAX = 25 is a placeholder for a §7.2 [TBD] that no module reads. It is kept green by tests/test_assumptions.py, which only checks that the module agrees with docs/assumptions.md — exactly the "prose about code fails by agreeing" pattern. The comment above it derives 25 two ways and reads as settled, so arriving at it from the module suggests a live limit rather than a dead constant. Contradiction check: I found none. Nothing in the §7.2 area asserts a value the spec contradicts. Two near-misses I decided were not contradictions: (a) ddn/lastmile/plan.py:22 claims allocation-by-count is safe because "§7.2 already penalises" unserved-in-the-same-place-every-day through the postponed-retry bullet — but nothing in this repository penalises it (row 7.2-2a), so the docstring's justification rests on a mechanism that does not exist; the code itself is not wrong, the reasoning is unbacked. (b) ddn/lastmile/plan.py:39-43 has a comment block…

**Notes — §9.1 Envelopes table.** SPEC VERSION: docs/vrp-problem-definition.md:3 now reads "Draft v0.12"; CLAUDE.md:14 still says "Draft v0.10". The §9.1 Envelopes table at lines 386-407 still carries the same 21 rows, so nothing in this group changed, but the CLAUDE.md pointer has drifted two revisions behind. THE BIGGEST FINDING — three non-optional §9.1 fields degrade silently when renamed. The stages read plain dicts, not the typed record (see row 9.1-record-is-not-the-runtime-path). In ddn/contract.py only package_id (line 240) and lat/lon (line 241) are hard subscripts. geocode_confidence (:175), priority (:195) and sla_date (:153) are read with .get and a default, and §9.1 marks none of them optional. Verified empirically, read-only, no files touched: renamed geocode_confidence -> routable: ['P1'] excluded: [] renamed priority -> tier/prize: (3, 0, 'COMMERCIAL') renamed sla_date -> tier/prize: (2, 15000, 'COMMERCIAL') Read in order: a low-confidence address is routed instead of flagged (§3.2 calls that "a leading cause of postponement"); every envelope collapses to the bottom band with prize 0, so prize-collecting stops discriminating; and §6.1's must-deliver-today hard constraint quietly becomes an ordinary commercial tier. Every test supplies the fields, so the whole 730-test suite stays green through all three. This is exactly the failure shape in the MEMORY note — the contract and the code agree because nothing forces them to disagree. contract.py:310-315 already argues this case for the vehicle capacity columns ("Absent is refused rather than skipped… a renamed one, which is the same thing from here") and refuses them; the envelope columns did not get the same treatment. ACCEPTED BUT NEVER USED: recipient_id is the only §9.1 envelope field with no reader anywhere in ddn/. It is declared twice, written by fixtures and notebooks, and read by nothing. TYPE DRIFT AGAINST §9.1: package_type is declared `enum` in §9.1 and typed bare `str` on both definitions (records.py:128, schemas.py:75), while every other enum column in the table has a real StrEnum. ddn/processing/readiness.py:33 documents why (the spec names no values) and invents ASSEMBLY_TYPES locally. Nothing constrains the accepte…

No file was created, edited or deleted. Scoreboard for this group: 25 IMPLEMENTED, 16 PARTIAL, 0 MISSING, 0 CONTRADICTS. Things that do not fit a row: 1. **The audit-quality asymmetry is the biggest finding.** `tests/test_contract.py:60` defines `contract_fields()`, which reads a §9.1 table *out of the document* so a rename in the spec breaks the test rather than passing silently. It is called exactly once — `test_contract.py:312`, for the Vehicles table. The Mailbags, Transfer-requests, Return-run-stops and Facilities tables have no such test. `tests/test_api_contract.py:128 test_the_mailbag_schema_is_section_9_1s_fields_exactly` looks like the equivalent but is not: it compares `ddn.api.schemas.Mailbag` against `ddn.model.records.Mailbag \| PickupRequest`, i.e. the repository against itself. Deleting `seal_id`, `assembly_required_count` and both `pickup_window_*` from both files leaves the whole 730-test suite green. That is the exact failure the docstring at `tests/test_model.py:1-6` warns about ("A check whose content comes from the thing it checks agrees with it whether or not it is right") and that the v0.10 `capacity_count` → `capacity_envelopes` rename already cost this repo once. Related: `SECTION_9_1_ENVELOPE` (test_model.py:48-54) is a hand-transcribed literal, not read from the spec — the comment says "Transcribed from §9.1's envelope table." 2. **Four §9.1 mailbag fields are inert**: `assembly_required_count`, `pickup_window_start`, `pickup_window_end`, `seal_id` — declared in `d…

**Notes — §9.2 Solver output.** SPEC VERSION: docs/vrp-problem-definition.md:3 says "Draft v0.12"; CLAUDE.md line 12 still says "(Draft v0.10)", and ddn/lastmile/plan.py:3 opens "v0.10's one-day lag". §9.2 is at line 456 as the task said, and the four bullets are at lines 458-461 — not 456-465 as the sed range implied (462-465 is the horizontal rule and the §10 heading). STALE CITATION, twice: CLAUDE.md:88 and docs/assumptions.md:142 both say the invented unreachable-address reason is at "lastmile.py:45". It is at ddn/lastmile/plan.py:43. There is no lastmile.py. Per the repo's own memory note ("grep the tree, not the diff"), this is exactly the prose-agrees-with-itself failure mode: the constant moved, both documents kept the old line. ORPHANED COMMENT above that constant: ddn/lastmile/plan.py:39-42 is a four-line comment explaining EFFECTIVE_PER_BIKE ("§7.4 ... The midpoint, used only to divide the fleet between facilities") and the line immediately under it, :43, defines UNREACHABLE_ADDRESS. EFFECTIVE_PER_BIKE now lives at ddn/allocation/fleet.py:21. So the invented §9.2 reason code is documented by a comment about a different constant in a different package — and plan.py:18 and :155 still refer readers to `EFFECTIVE_PER_BIKE` as though it were local. §5.3.2's "hub-origin loads for facilities still ahead on the circuit" is not modelled. ddn/linehaul/plan.py:230-231 keys hub_loads by the one facility the trip was chosen for and attaches it only to leg 0. A circuit that dropped envelopes at two depots could not be expressed, and nothing tests it. This is the same shape as the return_ids gap: the Leg dataclass is §9.2-complete, the planner that fills it is not. THREE untested branches in the §9.2 output path, all of them the discriminating ones: output.py:128-136 `_full` (the time/count distinction §9.2 asks for), circuit.py:149-151 MISSES_DEADLINE (the deadline reason §5.3.2 is about), and every per-leg field except weight_g. The suite is strong where the rule is arithmetic (§10's twenty unassigned, the load falling as the circuit drops it) and thin where the rule is a classification. WEAK ASSERTION PATTERN on route totals: `distance_m > 0` and `duration_s > 0` are the only checks on §9.2's…

**Notes — §5.2.6 Envelope lifecycle.** METHOD. Read-only throughout: `git status --porcelain` is empty at the end. Beyond opening the files and running tests in isolation, I generated per-edge evidence with two pytest plugins written to the scratchpad (never into the repo): `mutant.py` deletes one edge from `ddn.model.lifecycle.TRANSITIONS` at plugin-load time, `mutant_add.py` adds one. Running the full suite under each answers precisely the question the audit asks — would any test fail if this code were removed or inverted. Scratchpad path: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-lvalverdeb-TeamDev-ddn/0930109c-4291-4808-9d95-07e93a6e0a2a/scratchpad ("<scratchpad>" in the command fields above). THE SUITE'S LIFECYCLE TESTS LARGELY CHECK THE TABLE AGAINST ITSELF. tests/test_model.py:40-43 builds both parametrisations from TRANSITIONS: LEGAL is every edge in the table, ILLEGAL is every pair not in it. The two parametrised tests (25 + 264 cases, a third of the visible lifecycle coverage) therefore test `may`/`advance` against the table and cannot detect a wrong table. Demonstrated both ways: dropping Sorted->Ready leaves 730 passing; adding Requested->Ready, which the spec plainly forbids, also leaves 730 passing. The only spec-anchored test is test_every_status_is_named_in_section_5_2_6, and it checks state *names* against the document, never arrows. Nothing in the repository compares the transition table to the §5.2.6 diagram. That is the single highest-value gap here: the diagram is machine-readable (a fenced block with `→` arrows) and the test file already reads the spec file at line 33. SIX EDGES ARE CODE WITHOUT A TEST. Reconciled->Assembled, Reconciled->Sorted, Assembled->Sorted, Sorted->Ready, Dispatched->Rejected, Dispatched->Returned, plus Returned->Return run — each can be deleted with the suite still at 730 passed. Sorted->Ready is the notable one: it is the edge that makes an envelope a solver input, and no test touches it. THREE CONTRADICTS, ONE OF THEM BEHAVIOURAL. Two are extra arrows in the table (Transfer requested->Ready, Transfer requested->Return run), both argued for in the module docstring from §13.2 and §5.3.2 rather than from §5.2.6; the module is candid about them, which is why I read…

**Notes — §5.3.2 Transfers.** Suite state: `cd /Users/lvalverdeb/TeamDev/ddn && uv run pytest -q` -> "730 passed in 3.81s" (full tail: ".... [100%]" then "730 passed in 3.81s"). Every test cited above was also run in isolation and passed; nothing is skipped or xfailed in this area. Spec drift the task text flagged: docs/vrp-problem-definition.md line 3 reads "Status: Draft v0.12", but CLAUDE.md's second paragraph still says "(Draft v0.10)". ddn/solver_adapter/postcheck.py:73 already tracks v0.12 ("v0.12's transfer bullets (§5.3.2)"), so CLAUDE.md is the stale one. Prose-vs-code defect worth a fix (this is the MEMORY.md failure mode exactly — a docstring that agrees with the spec while the code does not): tests/test_simulation.py:413-422, `test_a_correction_that_changes_depot_raises_a_transfer`, says of the SLA-today envelope "so that envelope goes back through §5.5 instead of onto a van". It does not. ddn/simulation/day.py:267 merely `continue`s, no RETURN_RUN transition is ever made (`grep -rn "RETURN_RUN" ddn/simulation/ ddn/linehaul/` -> no hits), and the sibling test at tests/test_simulation.py:443 asserts `at["MRN-01761"] == ("D1", "D1")`, "the refused one never left". The two docstrings in the same file describe opposite outcomes; the second one matches the code. §5.3.2's "otherwise it is returned to the customer via the hub" is therefore unimplemented outside the state machine. Systematic gap: §5.3.2's whole Priority paragraph is absent from the line-haul stage. `grep -rn "priority" ddn/linehaul/` returns zero hits, and §9.1's transfer table (doc lines 425-433) carries no priority column either — so the data to rank on is not in the record. Today a 500 kg circuit silently prefers hub-origin envelopes over every transfer regardless of score, and declines transfers in list order. That is not a labelled fallback anywhere; it is just unwritten. Related: CLAUDE.md warns against layering SLA weighting on priority, which is a different concern and is respected — the gap here is that no ranking exists at all. Second systematic gap: the planner never reads `transfer.deadline`. `choose` plans against the receiving depot's morning release only (circuit.py:146-151), while §9.1's deadline is min(…

**Notes — §6 Outcomes and §6.1.** HEADLINE (the thing you asked me to hunt for): there is NO SLA weighting layered on top of priority anywhere. I grepped every `sla`/`SLA` hit in ddn/ (53) and every `priority` hit outside contract.py, and read ddn/lastmile/plan.py, ddn/solver_adapter/problem.py (which is a pure delegation to contract.to_problem, problem.py:45-51) and ddn/contract.py end to end. `_tier_and_prize` (contract.py:192-204) returns `score * PRIZE_SCALE` on all three paths; the SLA-today branch changes only `priority_tier` (0) and `priority_source` ("SLA"). `lastmile.select` sorts on `(-priority, package_id)` alone. So: no CONTRADICTS here. What IS missing is the guard that keeps it that way — no test pins the prize of an SLA-today package, so inserting a multiplier at contract.py:200 passes all 730 tests. That is the one-line fix worth making. THREE REAL DEFECTS FOUND, in descending order: 1. SLA-expired envelopes vanish from the simulation (row 6.1-expired-goes-to-return-run). §6.1 requires them returned to the customer via the return run. `_doorstep` seeds `going_back` with `list(attempt.expired)` (ddn/simulation/day.py:209) but never sets `sla_expired=True` or rewrites `previous_outcome`; `returns.eligible` (ddn/returns/run.py:92) therefore filters every one of them out, and `_handover` (day.py:398-402) does not carry them into tomorrow either. Verified by direct probe, output in that row. `report.tally.sla_expired` still counts them (day.py:420), so the metric reports envelopes the run silently dropped — the number looks handled. Nothing catches this because the only test for the flag (tests/test_returns.py:71) sets it by hand. 2. Depot rejections join tonight's return run instead of the following evening's (row 6-rejected-handling-back-to-facility, CONTRADICTS). ddn/returns/run.py:91 gates on `facility_id == "HUB"` for exactly the §5.5 reason, and tests/test_returns.py::test_a_depot_rejection_waits_for_the_following_evening proves the module honours it — but ddn/simulation/day.py:225-226 stamps `facility_id=hub_id` on every rejected/returned envelope at the doorstep, and day.py:367 stamps it again, so the gate can never fire from the pipeline. This is the failure mode MEMORY.md n…

**Notes — §8 Objectives, tiering, override, capacity gaps.** HEADLINE (three related findings, rows 8-weights-invented-in-the-model-file / 8-prize-scale-is-an-invented-weight-in-a-module / 8-model-objective-block-is-inert): CLAUDE.md and docs/assumptions.md:133 both say §8's objective weights get no placeholder at all. Two exist anyway. 1. models/ddn-lastmile.json:52-54 carries "vehicle_fixed_cost": 50000, "cost_per_metre": 1, "cost_per_second": 0, plus "prize": 1000 at :33. Identical blocks sit in models/ddn-pickup.json:57-59 and models/ddn-return.json:66-68. None is in docs/assumptions.md, so tests/test_assumptions.py cannot police them. 2. ddn/contract.py:110 sets PRIZE_SCALE = 100. That constant IS the envelope-versus-kilometre ratio — contract.py:86-87 says so in its own words ("A priority score and a metre are not the same unit, and the objective adds them"). It is hard-coded in a module, which CLAUDE.md forbids for exactly this class of value, and it is the single largest lever on which envelopes the solver declines. 3. Worse than either: the model weights are INERT. vrp.servicemodel.build returns a Problem with no objective (run.objective belongs to RunSpec, which DDN never calls), and all three DDN paths — contract._vehicle:326, solver_adapter.problem._van:120, returns.run._vehicle:130 — then replace build's vehicles with their own, leaving fixed_cost/cost_per_metre/cost_per_second at 0. Proven by construction: a model priced at 50,000 and one at 500 produce byte-identical Problem.vehicles. Distance is minimised only because PyVRP's VehicleType defaults unit_distance_cost to 1. That has three knock-on effects worth someone's attention: - ddn/api/runner.py:58-79 `_priced` is dead code with a docstring explaining what it does. An API caller who passes "vehicle_fixed_cost" (tests/test_api_peak_day.py:234 passes 500) changes nothing about the plan. - tests/test_solver_adapter.py:71-87 `deployable()` exists to price a bike so it is worth deploying, and does not. Every test built on that fixture is silently running the shipped pricing. The tests still pass — because with all costs at 0 and prizes at 180,000 the solver deploys anyway — which is why nobody noticed. - The sentence "models/ddn-lastmile.json prices a vehic…

**Notes — §5.1 Pickups and §5.1.7 exceptions.** SPEC VERSION: the task says Draft v0.12, but /Users/lvalverdeb/TeamDev/ddn/docs/vrp-problem-definition.md is the version CLAUDE.md calls Draft v0.10. I audited the file in the tree. §5.1 sits at lines 133-188 as the task said, so the section numbering lines up; I could not check whether v0.12 changed any §5.1 wording. THE BIGGEST GAP IS THE VAN DAY, NOT THE EXCEPTIONS. §5.1.7's five rows are the best-covered part of this stage: five incidents, five branches, seven tests, all passing in isolation and all of them would fail if the branch were inverted. What is missing is the fourth bullet of §5.1.5 — the van's own day. There is no mid-day hub return in ddn/pickups/dispatch.py at all: _Van.bags and _Van.grams are incremented at :243-244 and never reset, and the only homeward leg is added once after the tick loop at :152-153. So "returns when bag capacity is reached", "returns after [TBD] minutes idle", and "re-enters the pickup pool (multi-trip)" are all absent, and a van that fills up is finished for the day. Bags a second trip would have collected land in `unplaced` instead. VAN_IDLE_RETURN_MIN = 45 exists as a labelled placeholder at ddn/assumptions.py:92 with no consumer anywhere in the repo. "LEAST ADDITIONAL ROUTE COST" IS A NEAREST-VAN PROXY. ddn/pickups/dispatch.py:210-224 computes both the outbound leg and the return-home leg, uses the pair to test feasibility (back_at), then ranks candidates on the outbound leg alone — `candidates.append((leg, van.id, van))` then `min(candidates)`. The marginal cost of an append is leg + home - current_home; ranking on `leg` picks the nearest van, which is a different answer whenever two vans differ in how far the new stop pushes them from the hub. ddn/pickups/admission.py:136-144 is weaker still: `assign` takes the first feasible van in list order with no cost term at all. Neither is tested for cost behaviour — the only two-van test has both vans sitting at the hub, so all legs tie and the choice falls to the van-id tiebreak. Three docstrings (admission.py:25-27, admission.py:123, dispatch.py:15) say the real pricing is `vrp.quote.quote_insertion`'s job; that symbol is never called anywhere in ddn/. This is the CLAUDE.m…

## 2. Evidence rules

Every `IMPLEMENTED` row above was produced by an agent that opened the production file, opened
the test, and ran that test in isolation. The per-row `command` and its output are recorded in
the run journal; a representative form is:

```sh
uv run pytest "tests/test_solver_adapter.py::test_a_motorbike_over_35_envelopes_is_a_violation" -q
# 1 passed in 0.06s
```

### Full suite

```
$ make test
uv sync --extra dev --extra api
Resolved 126 packages in 4ms
Audited 77 packages in 10ms
uv run pytest tests/ -q
........................................................................ [  9%]
........................................................................ [ 19%]
........................................................................ [ 29%]
........................................................................ [ 39%]
........................................................................ [ 49%]
........................................................................ [ 59%]
........................................................................ [ 69%]
........................................................................ [ 78%]
........................................................................ [ 88%]
........................................................................ [ 98%]
..........                                                               [100%]
730 passed in 3.89s
```

### Test count against git history

| Source | Count |
|---|---|
| `make test`, this audit | **730 passed** |
| `pytest --collect-only` | **730 collected** |
| Last task's reported output (`96b5f2f`: *"Count moves 728 -> 730: two new tests"*) | **730** |
| `grep -rn "def test_" tests/` | 310 functions |

**The counts agree.** The 310→730 gap is `@pytest.mark.parametrize` (13 sites), not hidden
collection. The suite needs no gateway, no Redis and no routing data, as `CLAUDE.md` promises.

### The seven checks the brief asked for, answered directly

1. **skip/xfail/TODO in `tests/`** — exactly one skip: `tests/test_front_doors.py:59`, conditional
   on `_narrowed()` (a `-k`, `-m`, `--lf`, `--ff` or single-file run). No `xfail`, no `TODO`,
   no `FIXME`, no commented-out assertion anywhere in `tests/`. The skip is defensible — a subset
   run cannot know the suite's size — but it means the test that pins the documented test count
   never runs when invoked by node id.
2. **Assertions diverging from §10 / §5.3.2** — exactly one: `tests/fixtures/peak_day.py:88-90`
   holds `DISPUTED = 10` where §10 says 12, with `SPEC_DISPUTED = 12` kept beside it and the
   reason documented in the module docstring. This is disclosed, not hidden, and §10 is the party
   at fault (§5). A second, softer divergence: five of the seven morning-pool shares are scaled
   by the fixture because §10 states only two of them.
3. **Mocks or patches of `solver_adapter` outside `tests/`** — **none.** The only `monkeypatch`
   in the repository is `tests/test_api_behaviour.py:270-289` (an environment variable). Nothing
   in `ddn/` or `notebooks/` patches the solver. `ddn/simulation/day.py`'s `_served_everything`
   default is dependency injection with a documented rationale, not a mock.
4. **§10 literals in non-test code** — `ddn/simulation/on_road.py:77` hard-codes `POOL, BIKES =
   4550, 120`, duplicating two figures `ddn/assumptions.py` already owns, and `:191` prints a
   literal `25` rather than `ENVELOPES_PER_BIKE`. `ddn/returns/run.py:53` keeps `SERVICE_SECONDS
   = 600` while its documented placeholder `RETURN_STOP_MIN` has **zero readers** in the entire
   repository — still exactly the defect `CLAUDE.md` holds up as the cautionary example.
5. **Unreachable checks in the post-check entry points** — no function is dead, but two branches
   are **production-unreachable**: the `stage == PICKUP` guard (`postcheck.py:326`) is never true
   outside tests because neither production caller passes `stage`, which makes the three pickup
   bullets (van-only, mailbag capacity, bags-whole) test-only in practice; and
   **`check_day_constraints` has no production caller at all** (§7, gap 2).
6. **Test files with zero assertions** — **none.** Of 310 test functions, 17 contain no bare
   `assert`; 16 of those use `pytest.raises`, which is a real assertion. The seventeenth,
   `tests/test_notebooks.py::test_the_notebook_runs`, asserts by executing the notebook and
   letting the exception propagate — which is precisely what that test exists to catch.
7. **Silently-empty parametrize** — four parametrize sources are computed rather than literal.
   Two carry explicit anti-vacuity guards (`test_notebooks.py:25`, `test_assumptions.py:55`).
   Two do not: `tests/test_model.py:40-44` derives `LEGAL`/`ILLEGAL` from `ddn.model.TRANSITIONS`
   itself, so emptying that table would empty the tests that police it rather than fail them.
---

## 4. Mutation spot-check

Nine one-line changes to production code, each applied alone to a clean tree, the full suite
run, then `git checkout --` and a clean-tree assertion before the next. **A constraint whose
mutation the suite does not catch is `PARTIAL` at best.** Five were the brief's requirement;
four more were added to separate *mechanism untested* from *value unpinned*.

| # | §7.1 constraint | One-line mutation | Suite | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | A motorbike never carries more than 35 envelopes | `postcheck.py:224` `amount <= allowed` → `amount <= allowed * 100` | **3 failed**, 727 passed | ✅ caught |
| M2 | A van's combined load never exceeds 500 kg on any leg | `circuit.py:36` `MAX_LOAD_G = 500_000` → `5_000_000` | **730 passed** | ❌ **SURVIVED** |
| M2b | *(same, mechanism)* the `COMBINED_LOAD` check itself | `postcheck.py:428` guard disabled | **1 failed**, 729 passed | ✅ caught |
| M2c | *(same, planner)* the `OVER_CAPACITY` decline | `circuit.py:140` guard disabled | **1 failed**, 729 passed | ✅ caught |
| M3 | Only ready envelopes are assigned | `postcheck.py:272` guard disabled | **1 failed**, 729 passed | ✅ caught |
| M4 | An envelope is not dispatched after its SLA date | `postcheck.py:276` guard disabled | **1 failed**, 729 passed | ✅ caught |
| M5 | A van does not depart on line-haul until back and unloaded | `postcheck.py:503` guard disabled | **1 failed**, 729 passed | ✅ caught |
| M6 | A vehicle's route **starts** at its home facility | `postcheck.py:246` guard disabled | **730 passed** | ❌ **SURVIVED** |
| M6b | A vehicle's route **ends** at its home facility | `postcheck.py:250` guard disabled | **730 passed** | ❌ **SURVIVED** |
| M7 | A transfer must reach its destination before its deadline | `postcheck.py:442` guard disabled | **1 failed**, 729 passed | ✅ caught |
| M8 | *(control)* `ENVELOPES_PER_BIKE = 25` → `250` | `assumptions.py:38` | **15 failed**, 715 passed | ✅ caught |

### The three survivors

**M2 — §7.1's 500 kg leg limit has a tested mechanism and an unpinned value.** Raising
`MAX_LOAD_G` tenfold changes nothing the suite can see, because both tests that exercise the
bullet build their violating input *relative to the constant*: `tests/test_transfers.py:178`
uses `weight_g=MAX_LOAD_G + 1` and `:133` uses `grams=MAX_LOAD_G`. The violating input moves
with the limit, so the limit is free. M2b and M2c show the surrounding logic **is** covered —
what is missing is a single assertion that 500 kg is 500 kg. Nothing in `tests/` pins it
(`grep 'above 500 kg'` → 0 hits). Contrast the motorbike's 35, which *is* pinned literally at
`tests/test_returns.py:206` and `tests/test_contract.py:290,328` — which is why M1 was caught.

**M6 / M6b — §7.1's "starts and ends at its home facility" is enforced by code no test
reaches.** Both halves of `_home_bullets` can be disabled with the whole suite green. This is
not redundancy with the platform: I read `vrp/verify/verifier.py` — `_check_route` covers INV-3
(timeline), INV-4 (travel), INV-5 (load) and INV-6 (duration/shift window), and **no invariant
checks a route's start or end location.** `postcheck._home_bullets` is the only enforcement of
that half of the bullet, and it is unverified. The two tests that appear to cover it
(`test_a_route_that_does_not_start_at_its_facility_is_a_violation` and its shift sibling) assert
`ROUTE_WITHIN_SHIFT in bullets_in(...)`, and `INVARIANT_BULLET` maps INV-4 onto that same
string — every handmade route in that file has arrival times inconsistent with the test matrix,
so INV-4 fires and satisfies the assertion whatever `_home_bullets` does.

> The mutation harness and the tracing agents ran concurrently; one agent independently observed
> the M6 mutation in the tree and reached the same conclusion from the other direction.

---

## 5. Fixture fidelity

`tests/fixtures/peak_day.py` is a single module with three builders, not two files: there is no
separate `peak_day_transfers` fixture — `grep -rn transfer tests/fixtures/` returns **zero**
lines, and §5.3.2's transfer scenarios are built inline in `tests/test_transfers.py` instead.

The fixture is **good**. Its numbers are derived from §10's prose, its one deliberate divergence
is disclosed in the module docstring with the spec value kept beside it, and it documents §10's
own arithmetic failure rather than papering over it. I verified that arithmetic independently:

```
per-facility ready split   1600+850+650+550+400+320+180 = 4550   ✓ matches §10's stated 4,550
inflow less the holds      4800 - 200 - 40 - 12         = 4548   ✗ two short of §10's 4,550
motorbike allocation       48+24+18+14+8+6+2            = 120    ✓
end-of-day outcomes        2880+50+30+120               = 3080   ✓ = 3,100 - 20 unassigned
tomorrow's pool            4550+20+120 = 4690 vs 120x25 = 3000   → 1,690 ≈ §10's "~1,700"  ✓
```

**§10 contradicts itself by two envelopes**, and the fixture holds `DISPUTED = 10` against
`SPEC_DISPUTED = 12` to close its own books, saying so at `peak_day.py:10-17`. That is the
right call and the right disclosure. A second §10 self-contradiction the suite surfaces:
D2–D6 hold 48 bikes = 1,200 envelopes of capacity, but §10's own morning pool (3,100 less the
1,150 hub and 620 D1 shares it states) leaves 1,330 for those five depots — 130 more than they
can carry, so §10's "other depots clear their pools" cannot be true as written.

There is no `peak_day_transfers` fixture and no second fixture module. `tests/fixtures/` holds `__init__.py`, `peak_day.py`, `record_road.py` and `road.json.gz`. `peak_day.py` is one 461-line module with three independent builders over a shared block of §10 constants: `build()`/`load()` (the collection day's 4,800-envelope inflow), `morning_pool()` (the 3,100 delivered today) and `returns_pool()` (the 80 returned). `grep -rn transfer tests/fixtures/` returns nothing, and `tests/test_transfers.py` references `peak_day` zero times — §5.3.2 is tested entirely on ad-hoc single-letter depots. WHERE §10 IS INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT — two places, both found and documented by the repo rather than by me: 1. 4,800 - 200 - 40 - 12 = 4,548, but §10 states 4,550 Ready and its per-facility split independently sums to 4,550. "Other depots clear their pools" is 130 envelopes short of possible (row 10.31). Everything else closes: allocation sums to 120, ready split to 4,550, end of day 2,880+50+30+120 = 3,080 = 3,100 − 20, and tomorrow 4,550+20+120 = 4,690 against 3,000 ≈ the stated ~1,700. ## 7. Summary

### Counts by status

| Status | Matrix (§1) | Forensic rows (§3, §5, §6) | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IMPLEMENTED` | **164** | 37 | code plus a test that fails without it |
| `PARTIAL` | **156** | 43 | code without a discriminating test, or a fallback where the spec asked for the feature |
| `MISSING` | **25** | 6 | searched and not found; the patterns are in the row |
| `CONTRADICTS` | **12** | 16 | the code or its prose asserts something the spec forbids or denies |
| **Total** | **357** | 102 | |

**58 rows were downgraded by adversarial review** after the tracing agent had
marked them `IMPLEMENTED` or `PARTIAL` — a 16% refutation rate, which is the reason this audit
ran a refute pass rather than trusting first-pass traces.

The headline shape: **roughly 46% of requirements are genuinely covered**, 44% exist but are
not held in place by a test that could fail, and about 10% are absent or contradicted. The
suite is honest about most of what it covers — but coverage of a *mechanism* is repeatedly
mistaken for coverage of the *value* or the *wiring*, and that is where every top finding sits.

### The ten most consequential gaps

#### 1. §8 — the entire objective is disconnected from the solver

*Spec: §8, §8.1*

All three solve paths call `servicemodel.build`, which prices its generated vehicles from the model's `run.objective`, and then **replace every one of those vehicles** with a locally-built `Vehicle` that never sets `fixed_cost`, `cost_per_metre` or `cost_per_second` — `contract.py:402`, `solver_adapter/problem.py:116`, `returns/run.py:195`. `grep -rn 'fixed_cost\|cost_per_metre' ddn/` returns **only** `api/runner.py:74-78`, which edits the model dict that is then discarded. I verified by execution: a problem built from the shipped model reaches the solver with `fixed_cost=0, cost_per_metre=0, cost_per_second=0`. **Objective 3 — "minimise total route time / distance" — therefore carries zero weight**; nothing prices a kilometre, and only the shift duration bounds a route. Three artefacts describe the mechanism as live and are all inert: the `run.objective` block in all three `models/*.json`; `runner._priced()`, whose docstring offers callers an override of it; and `tests/test_solver_adapter.py:72-85`'s `deployable()` fixture, whose premise — "sets `vehicle_fixed_cost` to 50,000 … so the solver declines everything" — I disproved by solving with both models and getting the identical result, 6 of 6 served, `fixed_cost=0` in both.

**A fix touches:** `ddn/contract.py:330-342` (`_vehicle`), `ddn/solver_adapter/problem.py:120+` (`_van`), `ddn/returns/run.py` (`_vehicle`), plus whichever of `models/*.json`, `runner._priced` and `deployable()` survive the decision.

#### 2. §8's blocking unknown is filled anyway, in a module, and the guard test cannot see it

*Spec: §8, CLAUDE.md*

`PRIZE_SCALE = 100` (`ddn/contract.py:110`) is the operative envelope-versus-distance ratio and its own 24-line comment says so — *"A priority score and a metre are not the same unit, and the objective adds them"* — and calls itself *"provisional until §8 supplies real cost figures"*. `docs/assumptions.md:132-136` lists these weights under **Deliberately absent** with *"No placeholder"*. The test that exists to enforce that, `tests/test_assumptions.py:86-95`, greps **the variable names of `ddn.assumptions`** for `COST|RATIO|SLA_TARGET|_TARGET_RATE`. `PRIZE_SCALE` is in `ddn.contract`, and its name matches neither pattern, so it passes trivially. The next test in the same file proves the authors know model files are *"a second place to drift"* and holds `ddn-pickup.json` to the document for `MAILBAGS_PER_VAN` — no equivalent check guards the cost ratio. Note the interaction with gap 1: because the metre side is 0, the ratio this constant sets one half of is not actually a ratio against anything.

**A fix touches:** `ddn/contract.py:110`, `docs/assumptions.md`, `ddn/assumptions.py`, and `tests/test_assumptions.py:86-95` (which must assert a property, not a naming convention).

#### 3. §7.1's day-spanning bullets are never checked against a real plan

*Spec: §7.1, §13.1*

`check_day_constraints` — the half of the post-check that exists for the bullets one solve cannot see (one vehicle per *day*, arrival before dispatch, van unloaded before line-haul, combined leg load, transfer deadline) — **has no production caller.** `grep -rn check_day_constraints ddn/` finds its definition, its re-export, and a docstring at `api/routers/transfers.py:50` that actively claims it runs. Meanwhile `api/runner.py:123` (`linehaul_plan`), `:157` (`allocation_plan`) and `:170,175` (`simulate`) each return `"violations": []` as a **literal**, without calling either entry point. §13.1 defines an empty list as success, so `POST /linehaul/plans` reports a clean §7.1 result for a plan nothing checked. Line-haul is exactly the endpoint those five bullets were written for.

**A fix touches:** `ddn/api/runner.py:113-123` primarily; the `Day` assembly it needs already exists in `ddn/simulation/day.py`.

#### 4. §7.1's 500 kg leg limit: the value is free

*Spec: §7.1*

Mutation **M2**: `MAX_LOAD_G` 500,000 → 5,000,000, whole suite green. Both tests build their violating leg relative to the constant (`MAX_LOAD_G + 1`), so the limit can be set to anything. The mechanism is covered (M2b, M2c both caught); only the number is unpinned, and it is the number §7.1 states twice.

**A fix touches:** One assertion in `tests/test_transfers.py` pinning `MAX_LOAD_G == 500_000` against §4.1's "Van | 500 kg", as `tests/test_returns.py:206` already does for the bike's 35.

#### 5. §7.1's "starts and ends at its home facility" is enforced by unreachable-by-test code

*Spec: §7.1*

Mutations **M6** and **M6b** both survive the full suite. `vrp.verify` does **not** check start/end location — I read `_check_route`; it covers timeline, travel, load and duration only — so `postcheck._home_bullets` is the sole enforcement. The two tests that look like coverage pass on INV-4 noise instead, because `INVARIANT_BULLET` maps INV-4 onto the same `ROUTE_WITHIN_SHIFT` string the assertion looks for.

**A fix touches:** `tests/test_solver_adapter.py` — assert on the violation *detail* ("starts at … not its home …") rather than on bullet membership, for this and every bullet that shares a string with an invariant.

#### 6. The post-check asserts a completeness it does not have, and its prose miscounts the section

*Spec: §7.1*

`NOT_YET_ENFORCED = ()` with `tests/test_solver_adapter.py::test_nothing_is_left_unenforced` asserting it — the module formally claims all thirteen bullets are enforced, while three do not meet this audit's standard (gaps 4, 5, and the `[TBD]` mailbag limit). Alongside it, four stale prose claims that are all green: `postcheck.py:7` *"Seven bullets … Four are not"* (= eleven) and `:43` *"§7.1's eleven bullets"* against **thirteen**; `solver_adapter/__init__.py:4` repeating "eleven"; `check_day_constraints`' own docstring saying "four" while it checks five; and `postcheck.py:43` citing **`tests/test_postcheck.py`, which does not exist**. v0.12 took §7.1 from eleven bullets to thirteen and only the code followed.

**A fix touches:** `ddn/solver_adapter/postcheck.py` prose, `ddn/solver_adapter/__init__.py:4`, `CLAUDE.md:24-30`, and a test that reads the count from the spec rather than restating it.

#### 7. Return loads are declared everywhere and populated nowhere

*Spec: §7.1, §9.2, §5.5*

`Leg.return_ids` (`linehaul/circuit.py:52`) has exactly one occurrence in the repository — its own declaration. So §7.1's *"combined load across hub-origin, transfer **and return** envelopes"* is checked over two of its three components, and §9.2's line-haul plan, which must list *"return package_ids"* per leg, always emits an empty tuple. §5.5's rule that depot rejections ride the van back and join the **following** evening's run is separately contradicted: `simulation/day.py:225-226` and `:367` stamp `facility_id=hub_id` onto every returning envelope at the moment of the outcome, so a depot rejection is at the hub instantly and joins **tonight's** run — defeating the correct `facility_id == "HUB"` gate at `returns/run.py:91`.

**A fix touches:** `ddn/linehaul/circuit.py` and `plan.py` to carry returns; `ddn/simulation/day.py:225,367` for the day-boundary.

#### 8. Two §8 requirements have no implementation at all

*Spec: §8, §8.2*

**Objective 4, "balance workload across vehicles at the same facility"**: searched `ddn/` and `models/` for balance, workload, imbalance, equit, stdev, variance — zero hits; the only occurrence in the repository is the spec line itself. **§8.2's force-*out* direction**: only the lock-in path exists. The platform ships `FORBID_ORDER_ON_VEHICLE` and `FORBID_DEPLOY` (`vrp/model.py:553,557`), so the capability is available and unused; §8.2 says operations may force an envelope *"in or out"*.

**A fix touches:** `ddn/contract.py` (a second `Lock` kind), `ddn/api/schemas.py` and `routers/planning.py:106` for the endpoint; objective 4 needs gap 1 resolved before it can mean anything.

#### 9. §5.2.3's assembly queue ignores the two fields the spec says order it

*Spec: §5.2.3*

§5.2.3: where the clean-room queue exceeds capacity it is *"prioritised by envelope priority and SLA date"*. `grep -n 'priority\|sla' ddn/processing/*.py` returns **zero matches in the whole package**; `readiness.py:108-109` sorts by arrival then package id. §5.2.3 also calls assembly the likely bottleneck on high-volume days, so on exactly the days the rule matters the wrong 200 envelopes roll to tomorrow — and §10's worked example specifies that the 200 rolled are *"the lowest-priority ones"*.

**A fix touches:** `ddn/processing/readiness.py:108-109`, plus a fixture assertion that the rolled set is the lowest-priority one.

#### 10. The repository describes a spec version it is no longer at

*Spec: §14, §13*

`CLAUDE.md:11` and `README.md:6` both say **v0.10**; `ddn/api/app.py:57` publishes the OpenAPI document as `version="0.11"`; the spec is **v0.12** and the code implements v0.11's §13 service *and* v0.12's transfers. `docs/capacity-finding.md:3` and `docs/solver-capabilities.md:3` are also measured against v0.10. §14's own revision table lists 0.12 above 0.11 and 0.10, out of order. Related, and the reason this ranks: three lifecycle edges exist that §5.2.6 does not draw (`Ready → Return run`, `Transfer requested → Ready`, `Transfer requested → Return run`), and §5.2.6 says transitions not listed are illegal — each is argued in a docstring rather than in the spec, which is precisely the conversation a version bump is for.

**A fix touches:** `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `ddn/api/app.py:57`, the two `docs/` headers, and §5.2.6 of the spec (or `ddn/model/lifecycle.py:77,92`).

The pattern worth naming: **`ddn/assumptions.py` is a good registry with a blind spot.**
`tests/test_assumptions.py` holds it to `docs/assumptions.md` row by row, and that mechanism
works. But it only sees values that are *in* `ddn/assumptions.py`. Everything the audit found
hard-coded — `PRIZE_SCALE = 100`, `BANDS`, `SERVICE_SECONDS = 600`, `POOL, BIKES = 4550, 120`,
the three `models/*.json` objective blocks and their fleet sizes — lives outside it and is
invisible to the one test written to catch exactly this. `RETURN_STOP_MIN` is the proof: it was
added to the registry to stop `returns.py`'s 600 being invisible, and it has **zero readers** in
the entire repository, so the literal it was meant to replace is still the one in force.

---

## Appendix — method

- 28 agents: 12 tracing requirement groups, 12 adversarial refuters, 4 forensic tracks
  (tamper, fixture fidelity, spec drift, open questions). 1,203 tool calls.
- Every `IMPLEMENTED` row required the agent to open the production file, open the test, and run
  that test in isolation. Commands and outputs are in the run journal.
- The mutation harness (§4) and the empirical objective checks (gap 1) were run directly by the
  lead auditor, serially, against the working tree, each with a clean-tree assertion after
  restore. `git status` is clean; `HEAD` is `96b5f2f`, unchanged.
- **Not done:** the brief's `docs/claude-code-task-prompts.md` does not exist, so no requirement
  was traced to a build prompt. No fix was proposed or applied, per the brief.
